,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{72}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{9\times 8 \times 7}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel{9}\times 8 \times 7}{\cancel{9}} \\\\
&= 8 \times 7 \\\\
&= 56
\end{aligned}
$

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $35 \%$ of $160$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 35 \% \ \text{of} \ 160  \\\\
&= \dfrac {35} {100} \times 160 \\\\
&= \dfrac {35 \times 160} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5600} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {56 \times 100} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {56 \times \cancel{100}} {\cancel{100}} \\\\
&= {56} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$56$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 35 \% \ \text{of} \ 160  \\\\
&= \dfrac {35} {100} \times 160 \\\\
&= \dfrac {35 \times 160} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5600} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {56 \times 100} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {56 \times \cancel{100}} {\cancel{100}} \\\\
&= {56} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= 1200 + 37  \\\\
&= 1237 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6.3 \ \text{km} - 140 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 6.3 \times 1000 \ \text{m} - 140 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 6300 - 140 \\\\
&= 6160 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 6160 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 616000 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

$6160 \ \text{m}$

Which is the largest? 

To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

In this case, there are more decimals than fractions, so let's convert all the numbers into decimals.

$0.305, \quad \dfrac{1}{4}, \quad 0.3, \quad \dfrac{1}{3}, \quad 0.35$

$0.305, \quad 0.25, \quad 0.3, \quad 0.33, \quad 0.35$

The  largest number is $= 0.35$. 



</div>

<div class='workings'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$

$
\begin{aligned}

x             &= 58 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$58 \degree$

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F6E175B5-9571-4201-8E17-818A77EA6BAE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Sam and Alice have baked a cake.

Sam eats $\dfrac{1}{4}$ of the cake.

Alice eats $\dfrac{2}{5}$ of the cake.

What fraction of the cake have they eaten altogether? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{eaten together}&=  \dfrac{1}{4} + \dfrac{2}{5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{1 \times 5}{4  \times 5} + \dfrac{2 \times 4}{5 \times 4} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{5}{20} + \dfrac{8}{20} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{5 + 8} {20} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{13}{20}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{13}{20}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{eaten together}&=  \dfrac{1}{4} + \dfrac{2}{5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{1 \times 5}{4  \times 5} + \dfrac{2 \times 4}{5 \times 4} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{5}{20} + \dfrac{8}{20} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{5 + 8} {20} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{13}{20}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{13}{20}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F37ABF50-3392-4D3A-8B06-B6F2AC2D84EC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Matthew buys $2$ sandwiches and a cookie with a $\pounds 5$ note.

How much change does he receive?

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)

                   
<!--
Price List
Sandwich $\pounds 1.49
Cookie $\pounds 0.69
Ice Lolly $\pounds 0.49
Soda $\pounds 0.99
-->



</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the table,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Money spent}     &= 2 \times 1.49 + 0.69 \\\\
                        &= 2.98 + 0.69 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 3.67 \\\\
\text {Change received} &= 5 - 3.67 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 1.33
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.33$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the table,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Money spent}     &= 2 \times 1.49 + 0.69 \\\\
                        &= 2.98 + 0.69 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 3.67 \\\\
\text {Change received} &= 5 - 3.67 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 1.33
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.33$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E93C167E-C6A3-443C-8E8B-0832FB8A5BB9'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The Battle of Naseby occurred on $14 \text{th}$ June $1645$.

How many years ago will that be on $14 \text{th}$ June this year? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Aa per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The difference} &= \text{ present year} - \text{battle year}  \\\\
&= 2022 - 1645 \\\\
&= 377
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$377$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Aa per the question,

$

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1C00F2F2-8AF4-4FC9-9257-D3BF315B5AD8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Lucy takes $2 \ \text{hours}$ and $25 \ \text{minutes}$ to walk up a mountain and 
$1 \ \text{hour}$ and $45 \ \text{minutes}$ to walk down.

For how long does she walk altogether? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{ Total time taken} &=  2 \ \text{hrs} \ \  25 \ \text{mins} +  1 \ \text{hrs} \ \  45 \ \text{mins}  \\\\
&=  4 \ \text{hrs} \ \ 10 \ \text{mins} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text{hrs} \ \ 10 \ \text{mins}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{ Total time taken} &=  2 \ \text{hrs} \ \  25 \ \text{mins} +  1 \ \text{hrs} \ \  45 \ \text{mins}  \\\\
&=  4 \ \text{hrs} \ \ 10 \ \text{mins} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text{hrs} \ \ 10 \ \text{mins}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-75F310E6-BEA4-40F1-997F-C89D0ADB9052'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In the number $267{,}865$ how many times bigger is the value of the first digit $6$ than the value of the second digit $6$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{60,000}{60} \\\\
&= \dfrac{60 \times 1000} {60} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 2 \times 3 \times 5 \times 1000} {2 \times 2 \times 3 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 1000} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
&= 1000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{60,000}{60} \\\\
&= \dfrac{60 \times 1000} {60} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 2 \times 3 \times 5 \times 1000} {2 \times 2 \times 3 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 1000} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
&= 1000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1000$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B997CEC0-1CE9-4FA7-9498-ABBF735994C3'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Which is better value, $600 \ \text{g}$ for $\pounds 3$ or $500 \ \text{g}$ for $\pounds 2.75$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {First value}     &= \dfrac {600} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 200} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 200} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                        &= 200 \ \text {g} \\\\
\text {Second value}    &= \dfrac {500} {2.75} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500 \times 100} {2.75 \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500 \times 100} {275} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 5 \times 5 \times 2 \times 2 \times 100} {5 \times 5 \times 11} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5} \times 20 \times 100} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5} \times 11} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 100} {11} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2000} {11} \\\\
                        &= 181 \ \text {remainder} \ 9 \\\\
                        &\approx 181 \ \text {g}
\end {aligned}
$

Using the calculation above,

Better value $= 600 \ \text {g}$ for $\pounds 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{g} \ \text{for} \ \pounds 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {First value}     &= \dfrac {600} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 200} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 200} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                        &= 200 \ \text {g} \\\\
\text {Second value}    &= \dfrac {500} {2.75} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500 \times 100} {2.75 \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500 \times 100} {275} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 5 \times 5 \times 2 \times 2 \times 100} {5 \times 5 \times 11} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5} \times 20 \times 100} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5} \times 11} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 100} {11} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2000} {11} \\\\
                        &= 181 \ \text {remainder} \ 9 \\\\
                        &\approx 181 \ \text {g}
\end {aligned}
$

Using the calculation above,

Better value $= 600 \ \text {g}$ for $\pounds 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$600 \ \text{g} \ \text{for} \ \pounds 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-473B53F9-0065-4183-A92E-E456B6396E3B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the size of angle $x$:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Add an image with showing vertically opposite angles being equal *

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Vertically opposite angles are equal}$.


As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, the third angle is also $= x$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + x + 68      &= 180  \\\\
2x + 68         &= 180  \\\\
2x              &= 180 - 68  \\\\
2x              &= 112  \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {112} {2}  \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {56 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {56 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
x               &= 56 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$56 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_starts

rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_ends

rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle

rtq_abbr_md_formula_vertically_opposite_angles

As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, the third angle is also $= x$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + x + 68      &= 180  \\\\
2x + 68         &= 180  \\\\
2x              &= 180 - 68  \\\\
2x              &= 112  \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {112} {2}  \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {56 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {56 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
x               &= 56 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2D3F5E62-8FD7-4F33-8609-8208FED8B677'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Bilal wants to divide $235 \ \text{m}$ of string equally between seven kites. 
Work out how much string each kite would get, giving your answer 
to one decimal place. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac{235 \ \text{m}}{7} \\\\
&=  33.57 \\\\
&\approx 33.6 \ \text{m}\\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$33.6 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac{235 \ \text{m}}{7} \\\\
&=  33.57 \\\\
&\approx 33.6 \ \text{m}\\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$33.6 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-98FB3AC6-2728-458B-AA28-1E5EF428AAFB'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the obtuse angle between the hands of a clock at $5$ o’clock. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

In the clock from $12$ to $12$ it was $360 \degree$

In the clock from $12$ to $6$ it was $180 \degree$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{So from} \  12 \ \text{to} \  1  &= \dfrac{180}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{30 \times 6}{6} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{30 \times\cancel 6}{ \cancel 6} \\\\
&= 30 \degree \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{So, the obtuse angle for} \  5  \ \text{O'clock} &= 30 \times 5 \\\\
&=  30 \times 5 \\\\
&=  150 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$150 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

In the clock from $12$ to $12$ it was $360 \degree$

In the clock from $12$ to $6$ it was $180 \degree$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{So from} \  12 \ \text{to} \  1  &= \dfrac{180}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{30 \times 6}{6} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{30 \times\cancel 6}{ \cancel 6} \\\\
&= 30 \degree \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{So, the obtuse angle for} \  5  \ \text{O'clock} &= 30 \times 5 \\\\
&=  30 \times 5 \\\\
&=  150 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$150 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the highest common factor of $14$ and $42$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{factors of} \ 14  &= 1, 2, 7, \bold {14} \\\\
\text{factors of} \ 42   &=  1, 2, 3, 6, 7, \bold {14}, 21, 42 \\\\
\text{The Highest Common Factor of} \ 14 \ \text {and} \ 42 &= 14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

ABBR: $\text{Area of triangle} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area}     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 2 \times 3 \\\\ 
                &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {2} \\\\ 
                &= \dfrac {\cancel {2}} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\ 
                &= 3 \ \text{units}^2
\end{aligned}
$

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the difference in volumes of the two boxes?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{volume of cuboid} &= \text{length} \times \text{width} \times \text{height} \\\\
&= 6 \times 3 \times 3 \\\\
&=  36 \ \text{m}^3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{volume of cube} &= \text{Volume} &= \text{side} ^ { 3 } \\\\
&=  3 ^{3} \\\\
&=  27 \ \text{m}^3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The difference in volumes of the two boxes}&= \text{volume of cuboid} \  -  \ \text{volume of cube} \\\\
&=  36 \ - \ 27 \\\\
&=  9 \ \text{m}^3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9 \ \text{m}^3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

&=  3 ^{3} \\\\
&=  27 \ \text{m}^3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The difference in volumes of the two boxes}&= \text{volume of cuboid} \  -  \ \text{volume of cube} \\\\
&=  36 \ - \ 27 \\\\
&=  9 \ \text{m}^3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the bigger box’s volume is the volume of the smaller box?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of the bigger box’s volume is  the volume of the smaller box} &= \dfrac{\text{volume of cube}}{\text{volume of cuboid}} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{27}{36} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{3 \times 9}{4 \times 9} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{3 \times \cancel 9}{4 \times \cancel 9} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{3}{4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{3}{4}$

</div>

&=   \dfrac{3 \times \cancel 9}{4 \times \cancel 9} \\\\

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much of each ingredient is needed to make $18$ pancakes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Ingredient is needed to make $18$ pancakes = Each ingredient is needed to make $6$ pancakes $\times 3$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The plain flour needed to make 18 pancakes} &= \text{The plain flour used to make 6 Pancakes} \times 3 \\\\
&= 120 \ \text{gm} \times 3 \\\\
&=  360 \ \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The Eggs needed to make 18 pancakes} &= \text{The Eggs used to make 6 Pancakes} \times 3 \\\\
&= 3 \times 3 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The milk needed to make 18 pancakes} &= \text{The  milk used to make 6 Pancakes} \times 3 \\\\
&= 210 \ \text{ml} \times 3 \\\\
&= 630 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$360 \ \text{gm} \ \text{Plain Flour} ,  9 \ \text{Eggs} , 630 \ \text{ml}  \ \text{milk}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

Ingredient is needed to make $18$ pancakes = Each ingredient is needed to make $6$ pancakes $\times 3$

\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}

&= 3 \times 3 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The milk needed to make 18 pancakes} &= \text{The  milk used to make 6 Pancakes} \times 3 \\\\
&= 210 \ \text{ml} \times 3 \\\\
&= 630 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much of each ingredient is needed to make $8$ pancakes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{ingredients required to make 8 pancakes} &= \text{ingredients needed to make 6 pancakes} + \text{ingredients needed to make 2 pancakes}  \\\\
\text{As per the requirement} \\\\
\text{ingredients required to make 2 pancakes} &= \dfrac{\text{ingredients required to make 6 pancakes}}{3} \\\\
\text{The quantity of plain flour used to make} \ 2 \text{Pancakes} &= \dfrac{120}{3} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{3 \times 40 }{3} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{\cancel3 \times 40 }{\cancel 3} \\\\
&=  40 \ \text{g} \\\\\\\\
\text{The number of Eggs needed used to make} \ 2 \text{Pancakes} &= \dfrac{3}{3} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{3}{3} \\\\
&= 1 \\\\\\\\
\text{The amount of milk needed used to make}  \ 2 \text{Pancakes} &= \dfrac{210}{3} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{3 \times 70 }{3} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{\cancel 3 \times 70 }{\cancel 3} \\\\
&= 70 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{ingredients required to make 8 pancakes} &= \text{required to make 6 pancakes} + \text{required to make 2 pancakes}  \\\\
&= \text{The quantity of plain flour needed to make} \ 8 \text{Pancakes} \\\\
&= 120 \ \text{g} + 40 \ \text{g} \\\\
&= 160 \ \text{g} \\\\\\\\
\text{The number of Eggs needed to make} 8 \text{Pancakes} &= 3 + 1 \\\\
&=  4 \ \text{Eggs} \\\\\\\\
text{The amount of milk needed  to make} 8 \text{Pancakes} &= 210 \ \text{ml} + 70 \ \text{ml} \\\\
&= 280 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$160 \ \text{gm} \ \text{Plain Flour} ,  4 \ \text{Eggs} , 280 \ \text{ml}  \ \text{milk}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{ingredients required to make 8 pancakes} &= \text{ingredients needed to make 6 pancakes} + \text{ingredients needed to make 2 pancakes}  \\\\
\text{As per the requirement} \\\\
\text{ingredients required to make 2 pancakes} &= \dfrac{\text{ingredients required to make 6 pancakes}}{3} \\\\

&=  \dfrac{3 \times 70 }{3} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{\cancel 3 \times 70 }{\cancel 3} \\\\
&= 70 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{ingredients required to make 8 pancakes} &= \text{required to make 6 pancakes} + \text{required to make 2 pancakes}  \\\\
&= \text{The quantity of plain flour needed to make} \ 8 \text{Pancakes} \\\\
&= 120 \ \text{g} + 40 \ \text{g} \\\\
&= 160 \ \text{g} \\\\\\\\
\text{The number of Eggs needed to make} 8 \text{Pancakes} &= 3 + 1 \\\\
&=  4 \ \text{Eggs} \\\\\\\\
text{The amount of milk needed  to make} 8 \text{Pancakes} &= 210 \ \text{ml} + 70 \ \text{ml} \\\\
&= 280 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-998DABA8-00BE-4F0A-8D36-19CCA9E7D239'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Vale Youth Club has $126$ members. There are $20$ more boys than 
girls. How many girls are members of the Youth Club? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of boys $= a$

Let the number of girls $= b$

As per the question,

$a = b + 20$

Using this we get,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 126 \\\\
b + 20 + b  &= 126 \\\\
2b          &= 126 - 20 \\\\
2b          &= 106 \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {106} {2} \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {2 \times 53} {2} \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 53} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b           &= 53
\end{aligned} 
$

Number of girls $= 53$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$53$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of boys $= a$

Let the number of girls $= b$

As per the question,

$a = b + 20$

Using this we get,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 126 \\\\
b + 20 + b  &= 126 \\\\
2b          &= 126 - 20 \\\\
2b          &= 106 \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {106} {2} \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {2 \times 53} {2} \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 53} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b           &= 53
\end{aligned} 
$

Number of girls $= 53$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$53$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-168D08D9-86C1-42D7-8F94-A59D2E34B478'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A number sequence is made by taking the previous number, doubling 
it and then subtracting $3$.

The first three numbers in the sequence are:

$4, \quad 5, \quad 7, \quad \ldots$

What is the sum of the next three terms in the sequence? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The difference between the terms is $\times 2 - 3$. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC
{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{\times 2 - 3 }}  \\
4,  &&    5,  &&  7,  &&   \ldots, &&    \ldots,  &&  \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}    &= 7 \times 2 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 14 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 11 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}   &= 11 \times 2 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 22 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 19 \\\\
\text{Third missing number}    &= 19 \times 2 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 38 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 35
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The sum of the next three terms}  &=  11 + 19 + 35 \\\\
                                        &=  65
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$65$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note

The difference between the terms is $\times 2 - 3$. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{\times 2 - 3 }}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{\times 2 - 3 }}  \\
4,  &&    5,  &&  7,  &&   \ldots, &&    \ldots,  &&  \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}    &= 7 \times 2 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 14 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 11 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}   &= 11 \times 2 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 22 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 19 \\\\
\text{Third missing number}    &= 19 \times 2 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 38 - 3 \\\\
                               &= 35
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The sum of the next three terms}  &=  11 + 19 + 35 \\\\
                                        &=  65
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$65$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C5226DA6-11EF-48BE-8568-05BE0F93C04B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Insert the symbols $+, -, \times, \div$ into the boxes to make the calculations 
correct: 

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$
14 \  
\boxed{\phantom{2}} \  
2 \  
\boxed{\phantom{2}} \ 
3 = 31
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$14 \quad  \boxed{\green
{\times}} \quad  2 \quad  \boxed{\green
{+}} \quad 3 = 31$

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long is it since $10{:}32$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

APTQ,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time gap between} \ ( 10:32 ) \ \text{and}  \ ( 14:27 )\\\\
\text{Let}  \ (10:32)  \ \text{be initial time}  , \text{and} \  (14:27) \  \text{final time} \\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \ (11:00) \ \text{From initial time} &= (11:00 - 10:32) \\\\
&= 28 \text{mins} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \  (12:00 ) \  \text{From} \ (11:00) &= (12:00 - 11:00) \\\\
&= 1 \text{hrs} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become}  \ (13:00)\  \text{From}  \ (12:00) &= (13:00 - 12:00) \\\\
&=  1 \text{hrs} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \  (14:00 ) \ \text{From} \  (13:00) &= (14:00 - 13:00) \\\\
&=  1 \text{hrs} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \  (14:27 ) \ \text{From} \  (14:00) &= (14:00 - 14:27) \\\\
&= 00:27 \text{hrs} \\\\\
\text{Then add all the time difference between the time gaps} \\\\
&=  28 \text{mins} + 1 \text{hrs}  + 1 \text{hrs} + 1 \text{hrs} + 00:27 \text{hrs} \\\\
&=  3 \ \text{hrs} \ \ 55  \ \text{mins}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text{hrs} \ \ 55  \ \text{mins}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>APTQ,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time gap between} \ ( 10:32 ) \ \text{and}  \ ( 14:27 )\\\\
\text{Let}  \ (10:32)  \ \text{be initial time}  , \text{and} \  (14:27) \  \text{final time} \\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \ (11:00) \ \text{From initial time} &= (11:00 - 10:32) \\\\
&= 28 \text{mins} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \  (12:00 ) \  \text{From} \ (11:00) &= (12:00 - 11:00) \\\\
&= 1 \text{hrs} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become}  \ (13:00)\  \text{From}  \ (12:00) &= (13:00 - 12:00) \\\\
&=  1 \text{hrs} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \  (14:00 ) \ \text{From} \  (13:00) &= (14:00 - 13:00) \\\\
&=  1 \text{hrs} \\\\\\\\
\text{Time takes by the clock to become} \  (14:27 ) \ \text{From} \  (14:00) &= (14:00 - 14:27) \\\\
&= 00:27 \text{hrs} \\\\\
\text{Then add all the time difference between the time gaps} \\\\
&=  28 \text{mins} + 1 \text{hrs}  + 1 \text{hrs} + 1 \text{hrs} + 00:27 \text{hrs} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

Mr Green goes into a café and buys one pack of sandwiches, one 
biscuit and two cups of tea and is charged $\pounds 5.80$.

Mrs Plum goes into the café and buys two packs of sandwiches, two 
biscuits and one cup of tea and is charged $\pounds 6.50$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much would it cost for one pack of sandwiches, one biscuit and one cup of tea?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of a pack of sandwiches $= s$

Let the cost of a biscuit $= b$

Let the cost of a cup of tea $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t  &= \pounds 5.80 \\\\
2s + 2b + t &= \pounds 6.50 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t + 2s + 2b + t    &= 5.80 + 6.50 \\\\
3s + 3b + 3t                &= 12.30 \\\\
3 \times (s + b + t)        &= 12.30 \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \dfrac {12.30} {3} \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \pounds 4.10
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches, $1$ biscuit and $1$ cup of tea $= \pounds 4.10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of a pack of sandwiches $= s$

Let the cost of a biscuit $= b$

Let the cost of a cup of tea $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t  &= \pounds 5.80 \\\\
2s + 2b + t &= \pounds 6.50 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t + 2s + 2b + t    &= 5.80 + 6.50 \\\\
3s + 3b + 3t                &= 12.30 \\\\
3 \times (s + b + t)        &= 12.30 \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \dfrac {12.30} {3} \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \pounds 4.10
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches, $1$ biscuit and $1$ cup of tea $= \pounds 4.10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 4.10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much is a pack of sandwiches and a biscuit?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the above answer,

$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t  &= \pounds 6.50 \\\\
s + b + t    &= \pounds 4.10 
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t - s - b - t     &= 6.50 - 4.10 \\\\
s + b                       &= \pounds 2.40
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches and $1$ biscuit $= \pounds 2.40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the above answer,

$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t  &= \pounds 6.50 \\\\
s + b + t    &= \pounds 4.10 
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t - s - b - t     &= 6.50 - 4.10 \\\\
s + b                       &= \pounds 2.40
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches and $1$ biscuit $= \pounds 2.40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much is a cup of tea?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the above answers,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t  &= \pounds 5.80 \\\\
s + b + t   &= \pounds 4.10
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t - s - b - t  &= 5.80 - 4.10 \\\\
t                       &= \pounds 1.70
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of a cup of tea $= \pounds 1.70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the above answers,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t  &= \pounds 5.80 \\\\
s + b + t   &= \pounds 4.10
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t - s - b - t  &= 5.80 - 4.10 \\\\
t                       &= \pounds 1.70
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of a cup of tea $= \pounds 1.70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

or less than $18 \ \text{cm}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

*TODO:: Draw a diagram with two triangle and 1 rectangle which consist of only the shaded part of the question. Also show the calculated measurements. *

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of triangle} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area of the shaded shape}     &= \text{Perimeter of rectangle} + 2 \times \text{Perimeter of triangle} \\\\ 
                                    &= 2 \times 6 + 2 \times \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
                                    &= 2 \times 6 + \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
                                    &= 2 \times 6 + \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                                    &= 12 + 6 \\\\
                                    &= 18 \ \text{cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18 \ \text{cm}$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-33003293-7D63-4EEE-98E2-B0957B77805A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The difference between a third of a certain number and one quarter of the number is $5$. What is the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                     &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                 &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                               &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                                                     &= 5 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                     &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                 &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                               &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                                                     &= 5 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $p * 4 = 48$ find the value of $p$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
p * 4                   &= 48  \\\\
p^2 + (3 \times {4})    &= 48 \\\\
p^2 + 12                &= 48 \\\\
p^2                     &= 48 - 12 \\\\
p^2                     &= 36 \\\\
p                       &= \sqrt{36} \\\\
p                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
p * 4                   &= 48  \\\\
p^2 + (3 \times {4})    &= 48 \\\\
p^2 + 12                &= 48 \\\\
p^2                     &= 48 - 12 \\\\
p^2                     &= 36 \\\\
p                       &= \sqrt{36} \\\\
p                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $2 * q = 25$ find the value of $q$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
2 * q                &= 25  \\\\
2^2 + (q \times {3}) &= 25 \\\\
4 + 3q               &=  25 \\\\
3q                   &= 25 - 4 \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{21}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times{3}}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel3} \\\\
q                    &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

4 + 3q               &=  25 \\\\
3q                   &= 25 - 4 \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{21}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times{3}}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel3} \\\\
q                    &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $s * t = 10$ where $s$ and $t$ are positive whole numbers, what are 
the possible values of $s$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s * t           &= 10 \\\\
s ^ 2 + 3t      &= 10
\end{aligned}
$


We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.

A quick check shows that $1, 3$ and $2, 2$ meets the criteria above.

Let's verify for $1, 3$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 1 ^ 2 + 3 \times 3 \\\\
&= 1  + 9 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Let's verify for $2, 2$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 2 ^ 2 + 3 \times 2 \\\\
&= 4  + 6 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Possible values for $s = 1, 2, 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1, 2, 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s * t           &= 10 \\\\
s ^ 2 + 3t      &= 10
\end{aligned}
$


We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.

A quick check shows that $1, 3$ and $2, 2$ meets the criteria above.

Let's verify for $1, 3$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 1 ^ 2 + 3 \times 3 \\\\
&= 1  + 9 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Let's verify for $2, 2$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 2 ^ 2 + 3 \times 2 \\\\
&= 4  + 6 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Possible values for $s = 1, 2, 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1, 2, 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
